Lemma 26
.

\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau)=\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},v,\tau)
holds if
\mathit{H}\approx_{(a,i)}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
and
\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau)(a,i)=0
.

Proof.

By induction on
\tau
.
The base case is
\tau=\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\}
, which is trivial because
\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau)=\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},v,\tau)=\emptyset
.

The induction case is
\tau=\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})
with
\mathit{H}\approx_{(a,i)}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
,
\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau)(a,i)=0
,
(a,i)\in dom(H)
, and
if
\mathit{H}\approx_{(a,i)}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
and
\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau^{\prime})(a,i)=0
then
\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau^{\prime})=\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},v,\tau^{\prime})
holds.
When
R(y)=\mathbf{null}
or
R(y)\notin dom(H)
, this is straightforward.
Suppose
R(y)=(a^{\prime},k^{\prime})\in dom(H)
, where
(a^{\prime},k^{\prime})
is an address.

\displaystyle\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a^{\prime},k^{\prime}),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\{(a^{\prime},k^{\prime}+j)\mapsto\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}\}+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\land\left\llbracket{[j/z]r}\right\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a^{\prime},k^{\prime}+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime})

(by definition of
\mathbf{own}
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\{(a^{\prime},k^{\prime}+j)\mapsto\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}\}+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\land\left\llbracket{[j/z]r}\right\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a^{\prime},k^{\prime}+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime})

(by I.H.)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\{(a^{\prime},k^{\prime}+j)\mapsto\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}\}+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\land\left\llbracket{[j/z]r}\right\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{H^{\prime}}((a^{\prime},k^{\prime}+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime})

(by
\mathit{H}\approx_{(a,i)}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
and Lemma
25
)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},(a^{\prime},k^{\prime}),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))

∎

Lemma 27
.

\mathbf{ConOwn}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime})
holds if
\mathbf{ConOwn}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
and
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma})
.

Proof.

We show
\mathbf{Own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)(a,k)\leq 1\implies\mathbf{Own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime})(a,k)\leq 1
for any
(a,k)\in dom(\mathbf{Own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma))
.
By
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
,
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma})
and Lemma
20
, for any
x\in dom(\Gamma)

\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(x),\Gamma^{\prime}(x))\leq\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(x),\Gamma(x))

(24)

holds.
For any
(a,k)\in dom(\mathbf{Own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma))

\displaystyle\mathbf{Own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime})(a,k)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in dom(\Gamma)}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(x),\Gamma^{\prime}(x))(a,k)

(by definition of
\mathbf{Own}
)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{x\in dom(\Gamma)}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(x),\Gamma(x))(a,k)

(by
24
)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)(a,k)

From above,
\mathbf{Own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)(a,k)\leq 1\implies\mathbf{Own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime})(a,k)\leq 1

∎

Lemma 28
.

\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},pv,\Pi z.[(z-w)/z](\tau\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))=\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},pv\boxplus\mathit{R}(w),\Pi z.(\tau\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))
holds if
R(w)\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

If
pv=\mathbf{null}
or
pv\notin dom(H)
,
\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},pv,\Pi z.[(z-w)/z](\tau\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))=\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},pv\boxplus\mathit{R}(w),\Pi z.(\tau\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))=\emptyset
.
If
pv=(a,i)\in dom(H)
for some
a,i
, then

\displaystyle\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},pv\boxplus\mathit{R}(w),\Pi z.(\tau\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\{pv\boxplus\mathit{R}(w)\boxplus j\mapsto\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\land\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}(pv\boxplus\mathit{R}(w)\boxplus j),[j/z]\,\tau)

(by definition of
\mathbf{own}
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\{pv\boxplus j\mapsto\llbracket[(j-w)/j]\,[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\land\llbracket[(j-w)/j]\,[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}(pv\boxplus j),[(j-w)/j]\,[j/z]\,\tau)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\{pv\boxplus j\mapsto\llbracket[j/z]\,[(z-w)/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\land\llbracket[j/z]\,[(z-w)/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}(pv\boxplus j),[j/z]\,[(z-w)/z]\,\tau)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},pv,\Pi z.[(z-w)/z](\tau\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))

∎

Lemma 29

(Preservation of
\mathbf{ConOwn}
)
.

In this lemma, we assume that
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma})
holds for the register
\mathit{R}
and the type environment
\Gamma
.

1.

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto R(y)}\right\},(\Gamma_{1},y\COL\tau_{y}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x}^{\prime}))
holds if
\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma)
and

\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{2}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\tau_{y}\leq\tau
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\tau\approx\tau^{\prime}_{y}+\tau^{\prime}_{x}
and

x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{1},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{2})\cup dom(R)
.

2.

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto H(R(y))}\right\},(\Gamma_{1},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})\TREF^{r},\Gamma_{2},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x}))
holds if

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma)
and
\Gamma\leq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}+\tau_{x}\approx\tau_{y}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{y}\approx(\tau^{\prime})^{=x^{\prime}}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,\neq\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{y}\approx\tau_{y}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\models r\,>\,\mathbf{0}
and

R(y)=(a,i)\in dom(H)
and

x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})\cup dom(R)
.

3.

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H\left\{{(a,i)\hookleftarrow R(y)}\right\},R,\Gamma_{1}[x\hookleftarrow\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})][y\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{y}])
holds
if
\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma)
and
\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1}
and

\Gamma_{1}(x)=\Pi z.(\tau_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y)=\tau_{y}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\approx(\tau^{\prime})^{=y}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,\neq\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\approx\tau_{\ast x}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\models r\,=\,\mathbf{1}
and
R(x)=(a,i)\in dom(H)
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{y}+\tau^{\prime}\approx\tau_{y}
.

4.

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto pv\boxplus\mathit{R}(z)}\right\},\Gamma_{1}[y\hookleftarrow\Pi w.(\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}})],x^{\prime}\COL\Pi w.(\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{x}}))
if

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma)
and
\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y)=\Pi w.(\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y}})
and

\Gamma,w\COL\mathbf{int}\vdash\Pi w.(\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}})+\Pi w.[(w-z)/w](\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{x}})\approx\Pi w.(\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y}})
and

x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cup dom(R)
and

R(y)=pv
.

5.

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H\left\{{(a,0)\mapsto 0,\dots,(a,R(y)-1)\mapsto 0}\right\},R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto(a,0)}\right\},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},x^{\prime}\COL\Pi z.(\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid 0\,\leq\,z\wedge z\,\leq\,y-1\implies\nu\,=\,0\}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})))
holds if
\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma)
and

\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1}
and

\Gamma_{1}(y)=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\mathbf{int}\models r\,=\,((0\,\leq\,z\wedge z\,\leq\,y-1)\produces 1,\mathbf{0})
and
x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cup dom(R)
and
a
is fresh.

6.

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H\left\{{(a,0)\mapsto\mathbf{null},\dots,(a,R(y)-1)\mapsto\mathbf{null}}\right\},R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto(a,0)}\right\},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},x^{\prime}\COL\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})))
holds if
\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma)
and

\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1}
and

\Gamma_{1}(y)=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\mathbf{int}\models\textit{Empty}({\tau^{\prime}})
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\mathbf{int}\models r\,=\,((0\,\leq\,z\wedge z\,\leq\,y-1)\produces 1,\mathbf{0})
and
x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cup dom(R)
and
a
is fresh.

7.

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma_{1}[x\hookleftarrow\Pi z^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{x}})][y\hookleftarrow\Pi z_{{\mathrm{2}}}.(\Pi z_{{\mathrm{1}}}.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{\ast y}})\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})])

holds if
\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma)
and
\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(x)=\Pi z^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}.(\tau_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})_{x}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y)=\Pi z_{{\mathrm{2}}}.(\Pi z_{{\mathrm{1}}}.(\tau_{\ast\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{\ast y}})\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z_{{\mathrm{2}}}\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\vdash(\Pi z^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}.(\tau_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{x}})+\Pi z_{{\mathrm{1}}}.(\tau_{\ast\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{\ast y}}))\approx(\Pi z^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{x}})+\Pi z_{{\mathrm{1}}}.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{\ast y}}))
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z_{{\mathrm{2}}}\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,\neq\,0\}\vdash\Pi z_{{\mathrm{1}}}.(\tau_{\ast\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})_{\ast y}\approx\Pi z_{{\mathrm{1}}}.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr^{\prime}})_{\ast y}
and
H(R(y))=R(x)
.

8.

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma_{1}\left\{{x\hookleftarrow\Pi w^{\prime}.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{x}}),y\hookleftarrow\Pi w.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{y}})}\right\})
holds if
\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma)
and

R(x)=pv\boxplus\mathit{R}(z)
and
R(y)=pv
and

\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1}
and

\Gamma_{1}(x)=\Pi w^{\prime}.(\tau_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{x}})
and

\Gamma_{1}(y)=\Pi w.(\tau_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y}})
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash(\Pi w^{\prime}.[(w^{\prime}-z)/w^{\prime}](\tau_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{x}})+\Pi w.(\tau_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y}}))\approx(\Pi w^{\prime}.[(w^{\prime}-z)/w^{\prime}](\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{x}}))+\Pi w.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{y}})

9.

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto\mathbf{null}}\right\},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},x^{\prime}\COL\Pi z.(\tau\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})))
holds if
\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma)
and

\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\models\textit{Empty}({\Pi z.(\tau\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})})
and

x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cup dom(R)

Proof.

The proof of
1
.

From Lemma
27
, it is sufficient to show the following:

•

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto R(y)}\right\},(\Gamma_{1},y\COL\tau_{y}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x}^{\prime}))
holds if

1.

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma_{1},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{2})
and

2.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\tau_{y}\leq\tau
and

3.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\tau\approx\tau^{\prime}_{y}+\tau^{\prime}_{x}
and

4.

x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{1},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{2})\cup dom(R)
.

The total ownership is:

\displaystyle\mathbf{Own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto\mathit{R}(y)\},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau^{\prime}_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{x}))

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} &\sum_{z\in y:\tau^{\prime}_{y},x:\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto\mathit{R}(y)\},\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto\mathit{R}(y)\}(z),(y\COL\tau^{\prime}_{y},x\COL\tau^{\prime}_{x})(z))\\
&\qquad+\sum_{z\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto\mathit{R}(y)\},\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto\mathit{R}(y)\}(z),(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})(z))\end{aligned}

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} &\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\tau)&\\
&\qquad+\sum_{z\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(z),(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})(z))&\end{aligned}

( by Lemma
13
and Lemma
23
and the assumption
3
)

From Lemma
20
and the assumption
2
,

\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\tau)\leq\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\tau_{y})
.
Furthermore,
\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\tau_{y})+\sum_{z\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(z),(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})(z))=\mathbf{Own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}))
.
Given the premise
\mathbf{ConOwn}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}))
,
it follows that
\mathbf{Own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}))(a,k)\leq 1
for all
(a,k)\in dom(H)
,
which implies
\mathbf{Own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto\mathit{R}(y)\},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau^{\prime}_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{x}))(a,k)\leq 1
.
Thus,
\mathbf{ConOwn}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto\mathit{R}(y)\},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau^{\prime}_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{x}))
holds.

The proof of
2

From Lemma
27
, it is sufficient to show the following:

•

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto H(R(y))}\right\},(\Gamma_{1},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})\TREF^{r},\Gamma_{2},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x}))
holds if

1.

\mathbf{ConOwn}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}))
,

2.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}+\tau_{x}\approx\tau_{y}
,

3.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{y}\approx(\tau^{\prime})^{=x^{\prime}}
,

4.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,\neq\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{y}\approx\tau_{y}
,

5.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\models r\,>\,\mathbf{0}
and

6.

R(y)=(a,i)\in dom(H)
and

7.

x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})\cup dom(R)
.

Let
\Gamma
be
(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})
,
\Gamma^{\prime}
be
(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x})
and

R^{\prime}
be
\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto\mathit{R}(y)\}
.
Since
x^{\prime}
is fresh, the update to
R^{\prime}
does not affect any existing types in
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
Therefore, we need only consider
\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(y),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))
and
\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(x),\tau^{\prime}_{x})
.
From the assumption
5
,

\llbracket[0/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}\,>\,0
holds. Furthermore, because of the assumption
7
,

\llbracket[0/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R^{\prime}}}\,>\,0

(25)

holds.

\displaystyle\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},(a,i),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))+\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i)),\tau^{\prime}_{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\{(a,i+j)\mapsto\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R^{\prime}}}\}+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\land\left\llbracket{[j/z]r}\right\rrbracket_{\mathit{R^{\prime}}}>0}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{y})

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i)),\tau^{\prime}_{x})

(by definition of
\mathbf{own}
)

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} &\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\{(a,i+j)\mapsto\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R^{\prime}}}\}+\sum_{j\neq 0\land\left\llbracket{[j/z]r}\right\rrbracket_{R^{\prime}}>0}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{y})\\
&\qquad+\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i)),[0/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{y})+\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i)),\tau^{\prime}_{x})\end{aligned}

(by (
25
))

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} &\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\{(a,i+j)\mapsto\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R^{\prime}}}\}+\sum_{j\neq 0\land\left\llbracket{[j/z]r}\right\rrbracket_{R^{\prime}}>0}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{y})\\
&\qquad+\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i)),[0/z]\,(\tau^{\prime})^{=x^{\prime}})+\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i)),\tau^{\prime}_{x})\end{aligned}

(by the assumption
3
and type strengthening does not affect
\mathbf{own}
)

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} &\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\{(a,i+j)\mapsto\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R^{\prime}}}\}+\sum_{j\neq 0\land\left\llbracket{[j/z]r}\right\rrbracket_{R^{\prime}}>0}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{y})\\
&\qquad+\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i)),[0/z]\,\tau_{y})\\
\end{aligned}

(by the assumption
2
and Lemma
23
)

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} &\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\{(a,i+j)\mapsto\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R^{\prime}}}\}+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\land j\neq 0}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau_{y})\\
&\qquad+\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i)),[0/z]\,\tau_{y})\\
\end{aligned}

(by the assumption
4
and Lemma
21
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\{(a,i+j)\mapsto\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R^{\prime}}}\}+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\land\left\llbracket{[j/z]r}\right\rrbracket_{\mathit{R^{\prime}}}>0}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau_{y})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},(a,i),\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,i),\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))

(by Lemma
13
)

Thus,
\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto H(R(y))}\right\},(\Gamma_{1},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})\TREF^{r},\Gamma_{2},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x}))
holds if

\mathbf{ConOwn}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}))
.

The proof of
3

From Lemma
27
, it is sufficient to show the following:

•

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H\left\{{(a,i)\hookleftarrow R(y)}\right\},R,\Gamma_{1}[x\hookleftarrow\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})][y\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{y}])
holds
if

1.

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma_{1})
and

2.

\Gamma_{1}(x)=\Pi z.(\tau_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})
and

3.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y)=\tau_{y}
and

4.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\approx(\tau^{\prime})^{=y}
and

5.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,\neq\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\approx\tau_{\ast x}
and

6.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\models r\,=\,\mathbf{1}
and

7.

R(x)=(a,i)\in dom(H)
and

8.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{y}+\tau^{\prime}\approx\tau_{y}

Let
\Gamma^{\prime}
be
(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\left[x\hookleftarrow\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})\right]\left[y\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{y}\right])
,
\mathit{H^{\prime}}
be
(\mathit{H}\{(a,i)\mapsto\mathit{R}(y)\})
.
Since only the types of
x
and
y
differ between
\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
, we will consider the
\mathbf{own}
of
x
and
y
.
Let
H^{\prime}
be
\mathit{H}\{(a,i)\hookleftarrow\mathit{R}(y)\}
.
From the assumption
8
,
\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(x),\Gamma(x))(a,i)=1
.
By this,

\displaystyle\mathbf{Own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)(a,i)

(26)

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in dom(\Gamma)}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(z),\Gamma(z))(a,i)

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{z\in dom(\Gamma\setminus\{x\})}\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(z),\Gamma(z))(a,i)

\displaystyle\leq 1

That means for each
z\in dom(\Gamma\setminus\{x\})
,

\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(z),\Gamma(z))(a,i)=0\\

(27)

\displaystyle\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(x),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))(a^{\prime},i^{\prime})+\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\tau^{\prime}_{y})(a^{\prime},i^{\prime})

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash(\Pi w^{\prime}.[(w^{\prime}-z)/w^{\prime}](\tau_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{x}})+\Pi w.(\tau_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y}}))\approx(\Pi w^{\prime}.[(w^{\prime}-z)/w^{\prime}](\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{x}}))+\Pi w.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{y}})

Let
\Gamma^{\prime}
be
(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\left[x\hookleftarrow\Pi w^{\prime}.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{x}})\right]\left[y\hookleftarrow\Pi w.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y}})\right])
.
Since only the types of
x
and
y
differ between
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
, we will consider the
\mathbf{own}
of
x
and
y
.

\displaystyle\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},pv\boxplus\mathit{R}(z),\Pi w^{\prime}.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{x}}))+\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},pv,\Pi w.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{y}}))

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},pv,\Pi w^{\prime}.[(w^{\prime}-z)/w^{\prime}](\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{x}}))+\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},pv,\Pi w.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{y}}))

(by Lemma
28
)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},pv,\Pi w^{\prime}.[(w^{\prime}-z)/w^{\prime}](\tau_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{x}}))+\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},pv,\Pi w.(\tau_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y}}))

(by Lemma
23
and the assumption
6
)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},pv\boxplus\mathit{R}(z),\Pi w^{\prime}.(\tau_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{x}}))+\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},pv,\Pi w.(\tau_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y}}))

(by Lemma
28
)

Thus,
\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma_{1}\left\{{x\hookleftarrow\Pi w.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{x}}),y\hookleftarrow\Pi w.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{y}})}\right\})
holds if
\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma_{1})
.

The proof of
9

From Lemma
27
, it is sufficient to show the following:

•

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto\mathbf{null}}\right\},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},x^{\prime}\COL\Pi z.(\tau\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})))
holds if

1.

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma_{1})
,

2.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\models\textit{Empty}({\Pi z.(\tau\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})})
and

3.

x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cup dom(R)

This follows immediately from the
Empty
constraint.
∎

Lemma 30
.

\mathbf{SAT}(H,R\left\{{x\mapsto n}\right\},(\Gamma,x\COL\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\}))

if
\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma)
,
\models[R][n/\nu]\varphi
,
\Gamma\vdash\varphi\mbox{ ok}
and
x\notin dom(R)
.

Proof.

We show that for any
y\in dom(\Gamma,x\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\})
,
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto n\},\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto n\}(y),(\Gamma,x\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\})(y))
holds
if
\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma)
,
\models[\mathit{R}]\,[n/\nu]\varphi
,
\Gamma\vdash\varphi\mbox{ ok}
and
x\notin dom(R)

by induction of
\Gamma(y)
.
The base case is
\Gamma(y)=\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi^{\prime}\}
.
If
y\in dom(\Gamma)
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)

\displaystyle\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\Gamma(y))

\displaystyle\iff\models[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{R}(y)/\nu]\varphi^{\prime}

\displaystyle\implies\models[\mathit{R}]\,[n/x]\,[\mathit{R}(y)/\nu]\varphi^{\prime}

(non-appearance of
x
in
\varphi^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle\iff\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto n\},\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto n\}(y),(\Gamma,x\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\})(y))

If
y=x
,

\displaystyle\models[\mathit{R}]\,[n/x]\,\varphi

\displaystyle\iff\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto n\},\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto n\}(x),(\Gamma,x\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\})(x))

The inductive case is the case where
\Gamma(y)=\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})
.
If
R(y)=\mathbf{null}
, this is straightforward.
We consider the case where
R(y)
is of the form
(a^{\prime},k^{\prime})
for some base address
a^{\prime}
and some offset
k^{\prime}

with
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto n\},\mathit{H}((a^{\prime},k^{\prime}+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime})
if
\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
,
\models[R][n/\nu]\varphi
,

\Gamma\vdash\varphi\mbox{ ok}
and
x\notin dom(R)
.

\displaystyle\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)

\displaystyle\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\Gamma(y))

\displaystyle\iff\forall j.\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0\implies(a^{\prime},k^{\prime}+j)\in dom(H)\wedge\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a^{\prime},k^{\prime}+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime})

(by definition of
\mathbf{SATv}
)

\displaystyle\implies\forall j.\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto n\}}>0\implies(a^{\prime},k^{\prime}+j)\in dom(H)\wedge\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a^{\prime},k^{\prime}+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime})

(non-appearance of
x
in
r
)

\displaystyle\implies\forall j.\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto n\}}>0\implies(a^{\prime},k^{\prime}+j)\in dom(H)\wedge\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto n\},\mathit{H}((a^{\prime},k^{\prime}+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime})

(by I.H.)

\displaystyle\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto n\},\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto n\}(y),(\Gamma,x\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\})(y))

∎

Lemma 31
.

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau)
holds if

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto v^{\prime}\},\mathit{v},\tau)
and
x\notin\mathbf{FV}\,(\tau)\cup dom(R)
.

Proof.

By induction of
\tau
.
The base case is
\tau=\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\}
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto v^{\prime}\},n,\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\})

\displaystyle\iff\models[\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto v^{\prime}\}]\,([n/\nu]\varphi)

(by definition of
\mathbf{SATv}
)

\displaystyle\iff\models[\mathit{R}]\,[v^{\prime}/x]([n/\nu]\varphi)

(by definition of
[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi
)

\displaystyle\iff\models[\mathit{R}]\,([n/\nu]\varphi)

(non-appearance of
x
in
\varphi
)

\displaystyle\iff\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},n,\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\})

The inductive case is the case where
\tau=\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})
with

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))
holds if

\displaystyle\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto v^{\prime}\},\mathit{v},\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))and

(28)

\displaystyle x\notin\mathbf{FV}\,(\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))\cup dom(R).

(29)

If
R(y)=\mathbf{null}
, this is straightforward.
Suppose
R(y)=(a,k)
for some
a
and
k
.
Since
x
is fresh,

\llbracket r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}=\llbracket r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto v^{\prime}\}}.

(30)

From (
30
), the premise:
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto v^{\prime}\},(a,k),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))

and the definition of
\mathbf{SATv}
, for any
j\in\llbracket r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}
,

\displaystyle(a,k+j)\in dom(H)

(31)

\displaystyle\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x\mapsto v^{\prime}\},\mathit{H}((a,k+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}).

(32)

By I.H. and (
32
), we get

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,k+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}).

(33)

By definition of
\mathbf{SATv}
, (
31
) and
(
33
),
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,k)),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))
is true.
∎

Lemma 32
.

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau)
holds if

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau^{\prime})
and
\Gamma\vdash\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau
and
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma})
.

Proof.

By induction of
\tau
.
The base case is
\tau=\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\}
and
\tau^{\prime}=\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi^{\prime}\}

with
\varphi^{\prime}\produces\varphi
.

\displaystyle\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},n,\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi^{\prime}\})

\displaystyle\iff\models[\mathit{R}]\,[n/\nu]\varphi^{\prime}

(by definition of
\mathbf{SATv}
and
[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi
)

\displaystyle\implies\models[\mathit{R}]\,([n/\nu]\varphi)

(by
\varphi^{\prime}\implies\varphi
)

\displaystyle\iff\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},n,\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\})

The inductive case is the case where
\tau=\Pi z.(\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
and

\tau^{\prime}=\Pi z.(\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
with
\Gamma,z\COL\mathbf{int}\models r_{{\mathrm{2}}}\,\geq\,r_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},w\COL\mathbf{int}\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.
If
v=\mathbf{null}
, this is straightforward.
Suppose
v=(a,k)
, where
(a,k)
is address such that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),\tau^{\prime})
.
We consider an arbitrary integer
j
where
\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0
.
From
\Gamma,z\COL\mathbf{int}\models r_{{\mathrm{2}}}\,\geq\,r_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and Lemma
16
, it follows that

\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0.

(34)

Since

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),\tau^{\prime})
,

(a,k+j)\in dom(H)
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,k+j)),[j/z]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
is true.
By I.H., we get

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,k+j)),[j/z]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}).

(35)

We proved that for any
j
where
\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0
,
both
(a,k+j)\in dom(H)
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,k+j)),[j/z]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
are true.
This is equivalent to
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau)

∎

Lemma 33
.

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau)\iff\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau^{\prime})
if
\Gamma\vdash\tau^{\prime}\approx\tau
and
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma})
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from Lemma
32
.
∎

Lemma 34
.

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{y\mapsto pv\},\mathit{v},\tau)
if
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau)
and
y\notin dom(R)
.

Proof.

Since
R(y)
is an address,
it follows that the update from
R
to
\mathit{R}\{y\mapsto pv\}
does not affect the ownership terms or refinement types within
\tau
.
∎

Lemma 35
.

If
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\approx\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}
and
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma})
, then

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})\wedge\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})\iff\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}})
.

Proof.

By induction of
\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}
.
The base case is
\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}=\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{3}}}\}
with
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\}
,
\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}
,

\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma})
and

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\approx\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}
for some
\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}},\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}},\varphi_{{\mathrm{3}}}
.

\displaystyle\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},n,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})\wedge\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},n,\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})

\displaystyle\iff\models[\mathit{R}]\,[n/\nu]\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\wedge\models[\mathit{R}]\,[n/\nu]\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}

\displaystyle\iff\models[\mathit{R}]\,[n/\nu](\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\wedge\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}})

\displaystyle\iff\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}})

The inductive case is the case where
\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}=\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr_{{\mathrm{3}}}})
with

\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
,
\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
,
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma})
,

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\approx\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})\wedge\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\iff\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})

for some
\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}},r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}
.
When
v=\mathbf{null}
, this is straightforward.
We consider the case where
v\neq\mathbf{null}
.

•

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})\wedge\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})\impliedby\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}})
direction

Assume
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr_{{\mathrm{3}}}}))
for some address
(a,k)
.

\displaystyle\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt3}))

\displaystyle\iff\forall j.\llbracket[j/z](r_{{\mathrm{3}}})\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0\produces(a,k+j)\in dom(H)\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,k+j)),[j/z]\,(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}}))

(be definition of
\mathbf{SATv}
)

\displaystyle\iff\forall j.\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0\lor\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0\produces(a,k+j)\in dom(H)

\displaystyle\qquad\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,k+j)),[j/z]\,(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}))\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,k+j)),[j/z]\,(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}))

(by I.H. and
\llbracket r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}
is non-negative)

\displaystyle\implies\left\{\forall j.\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0\lor\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0\produces(a,k+j)\in dom(H)\right.\left.\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,k+j)),[j/z]\,(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}))\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\land\left\{\forall j.\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0\lor\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0\produces(a,k+j)\in dom(H)\right.\land\left.\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,k+j)),[j/z]\,(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}))\right\}

\displaystyle\implies\left\{\forall j.\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0\produces(a,k+j)\in dom(H)\right.\land\left.\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,k+j)),[j/z]\,(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}))\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\land\left\{\forall j.\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0\produces(a,k+j)\in dom(H)\right.\land\left.\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,k+j)),[j/z]\,(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}))\right\}

\displaystyle\iff\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr_{{\mathrm{1}}}}))\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr_{{\mathrm{2}}}}))

•

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})\wedge\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})\produces\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})

Assume
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr_{{\mathrm{1}}}}))\wedge\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr_{{\mathrm{2}}}}))
for some address
(a,k)
.
First, we show
(a,k+j)\in dom(H)
.
For any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{3}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0
,

\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{3}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}=\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}+\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}
holds by Lemma
15
.
If their sum is positive, then either

\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0\lor\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0
.
If
\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}
, the initial premise of
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr_{{\mathrm{1}}}}))
implies
(a,k+j)\in dom(H)
.
If
\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}
, the initial premise of
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr_{{\mathrm{2}}}}))
implies
(a,k+j)\in dom(H)
.
In either case, we get
(a,k+j)\in dom(H)
.

Next, we show
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})\wedge\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})
to use I.H..
If
\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0
, then
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})
is true by assumption.
If
\llbracket[j/z]r_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}=0
, the well-formedness rules require that
\Gamma\models\textit{Empty}({[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
is true
, for which
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})
holds.
Thus,
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})
is always true.
We can show
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})
to use the exact same reasoning.
Since the premise for the I.H. is satisfied,
we can apply it to conclude that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k+j),[j/z]\,(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}}))
holds.
Because
(a,k+j)\in dom(H)
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,k+j),[j/z]\,(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}}))
are true for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\llbracket[j/z](r_{{\mathrm{3}}})\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0
,

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}})
holds.
∎

Lemma 36
.

\models[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi
holds if

\models[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi^{\prime}
and
\Gamma\models\varphi\implies\varphi^{\prime}
and
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma})
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\Gamma\models(\varphi\implies\varphi^{\prime})

\displaystyle\models(\textit{fml}({\Gamma})\implies\varphi\implies\varphi^{\prime})

\displaystyle\iff\models([\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma})\implies[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi\implies[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi^{\prime})

\displaystyle\iff\models([\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma}))\implies\models([\mathit{R}]\,\varphi\implies[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi^{\prime})

\displaystyle\implies\models([\mathit{R}]\,\varphi\implies[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi^{\prime})

(by
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma})
)

\displaystyle\iff\models([\mathit{R}]\,\varphi)\implies\models([\mathit{R}]\,\varphi^{\prime})

Thus,
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi
holds if

\models[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 37
.

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime})
holds if

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
and
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma})
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from Lemma
32
.
∎

Lemma 38
.

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau)
holds if
\tau
is of the form
\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})
,

\Gamma\models\textit{Empty}({\tau})
,
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma})
and
v
is
\mathbf{null}
or
(a.i)
for some
\tau^{\prime},r,a,i
.

Proof.

By
\Gamma\models\textit{Empty}({\tau})
,
\forall j.\left\llbracket{r}\right\rrbracket_{R\{z\mapsto j\}}=0
.
From this and the definition of
\mathbf{SATv}
, this lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 39
.

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},v,\tau)
holds if
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau)
,
\mathit{H}\approx_{(a,i)}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
and
\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau)(a,i)=0
.

Proof.

By induction of
\tau
.
The base case is
\tau=\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\}
and
v
is integer
n
, which is trivial
because
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},n,\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\})\iff\models[\mathit{R}]\,[n/\nu]\varphi\iff\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},n,\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\})
.

The inductive case is the case where
\tau=\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})

with
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau)
,
\mathit{H}\approx_{(a,i)}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
,
\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau)(a,i)=0
and
if
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},v,\tau^{\prime})
,
\mathit{H}\approx_{(a,i)}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
and
\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},v,\tau^{\prime})(a,i)=0
then
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},v,\tau^{\prime})
.
When
v=\mathbf{null}
, this is straightforward.
Suppose
v=(a^{\prime},i^{\prime})
, where
(a^{\prime},i^{\prime})
is an address.
By
\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a^{\prime},i^{\prime}),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))(a,i)=0
,
We have

\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}.\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0\implies\mathbf{own}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a^{\prime},i^{\prime})),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime})(a,i)=0.

(36)

Therefore

\displaystyle\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a^{\prime},i^{\prime}),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))

\displaystyle\iff\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}.\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0\implies(a^{\prime},k^{\prime}+j)\in dom(H)\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a^{\prime},i^{\prime}+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime})

\displaystyle\implies\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}.\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0\implies(a^{\prime},k^{\prime}+j)\in dom(H)\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a^{\prime},i^{\prime}+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime})

(by
36
and I.H.)

\displaystyle\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},(a^{\prime},i^{\prime}),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))

Thus,
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},v,\tau)
holds if
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau)
.
∎

Lemma 40

(Preservation of
\mathbf{SAT}
)
.

1.

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto\mathit{R}(y)\},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau^{\prime}_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{x}))
holds if
\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma)
and

\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{2}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\tau_{y}\leq\tau
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\tau\approx\tau^{\prime}_{y}+\tau^{\prime}_{x}
and

x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{1},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{2})\cup dom(R)
.

2.

If

\Gamma\leq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x}
and

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})
,

(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\})\vdash\tau^{\prime}+\tau_{x}\approx\tau_{y}
,

(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\})\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{y}\approx(\tau^{\prime})^{=x^{\prime}}
,

(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,\neq\,0\})\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{y}\approx\tau_{y}
,

x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})\cup dom(R)
,
R(y)=(a,i)\in dom(H)

and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\models r\,>\,\mathbf{0}
, then

\mathbf{SAT}(H,R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto H(R(y))}\right\},\Gamma)\cup dom(R)
.

3.

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H}\{(a,i)\hookleftarrow\mathit{R}(y)\},\mathit{R},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\left[x\hookleftarrow\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})\right]\left[y\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{y}\right])
holds if

\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma)
and
\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(x)=\Pi z.(\tau_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y)=\tau_{y}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\approx(\tau^{\prime})^{=y}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,\neq\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\approx\tau_{\ast x}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\models r\,=\,\mathbf{1}

and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{y}+\tau^{\prime}\approx\tau_{y}

and
R(x)=(a,i)\in dom(H)
.

4.

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto pv\boxplus\mathit{R}(z)\},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\left[y\hookleftarrow\Pi w.(\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}})\right],x^{\prime}\COL\Pi w.(\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{x}}))
holds if
\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma)
and

\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y)=\Pi w.(\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y}})
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(z)=\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\}
and
R(y)=(a,i)
and

\Gamma,w\COL\mathbf{int}\vdash\Pi w.(\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}})+\Pi w.[(w-z)/w](\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{x}})\approx\Pi w.(\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y}})
and

x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cup dom(R)

5.

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H}\{(a,0)\mapsto 0,\ldots,(a,\mathit{R}(y)\,-\,1)\mapsto 0\},\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto(a,0)\},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},x^{\prime}\COL\Pi z.(\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid 0\,\leq\,z\wedge z\,\leq\,y-1\implies\nu\,=\,0\}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})))
holds if

\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma)
and
\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y)=\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\mathbf{int}\models r\,=\,((0\,\leq\,z\wedge z\,\leq\,y-1)\produces 1,\mathbf{0})

and
x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cup dom(R)
and
a
is fresh.

6.

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H}\{(a,0)\mapsto\mathbf{null},\ldots,(a,\mathit{R}(y)\,-\,1)\mapsto\mathbf{null}\},\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto(a,0)\},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},x^{\prime}\COL\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})))
holds if
\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma)
and

\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1}
and

\Gamma_{1}(y)=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\mathbf{int}\models\textit{Empty}({\tau^{\prime}})
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\mathbf{int}\models r\,=\,((0\,\leq\,z\wedge z\,\leq\,y-1)\produces 1,\mathbf{0})
.
and
x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cup dom(R)
and
a
is fresh.

7.

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\left[x\hookleftarrow\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{x}})\right]\left[y\hookleftarrow\Pi w.(\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{\ast y}})\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})\right])
holds if

\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma)
and
\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(x)=\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{x}})
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y)=\Pi w.(\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{\ast y}})\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})
and

\Gamma,w\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\vdash(\Pi z.(\tau_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{x}})+\Pi z.(\tau_{\ast\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{\ast y}}))\approx(\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{x}})+\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{\ast y}}))
and

\Gamma,w\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,\neq\,0\}\vdash\Pi z.(\tau_{\ast\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{\ast y}})\approx\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{\ast y}})

and
H(R(y))=R(x)
.

8.

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\left[x\hookleftarrow\Pi w^{\prime}.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{x}})\right]\left[y\hookleftarrow\Pi w.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{y}})\right])

holds if
\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma)
and

R(x)=pv\boxplus\mathit{R}(z)
and
R(y)=pv
and

\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(x)=\Pi w^{\prime}.(\tau_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{x}})
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y)=\Pi w.(\tau_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y}})
and

\Gamma\vdash(\Pi w^{\prime}.[(w^{\prime}-z)/w^{\prime}](\tau_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{x}})+\Pi w.(\tau_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y}}))\approx(\Pi w^{\prime}.[(w^{\prime}-z)/w^{\prime}](\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{x}}))+\Pi w.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{y}})

9.

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},x^{\prime}\COL\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})))
holds if
\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma)
and

\Gamma\subt\Gamma_{1}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\mathbf{int}\models\textit{Empty}({\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})})
and

x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cup dom(R)

Proof.

The proof of
1
.
From Lemma
37
, it is sufficient to show the following:

•

\mathbf{SAT}(H,R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto R(y)}\right\},(\Gamma_{1},y\COL\tau_{y}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x}^{\prime}))
holds if

1.

\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma_{1},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{2})
and

2.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\tau_{y}\leq\tau
and

3.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\tau\approx\tau^{\prime}_{y}+\tau^{\prime}_{x}
and

4.

x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{1},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{2})\cup dom(R)
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime}
be
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto\mathit{R}(y)\}
be
\mathit{R^{\prime}}
.

\displaystyle\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime})

\displaystyle\iff\forall x\in dom(\Gamma^{\prime}).\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(x),\Gamma^{\prime}(x))

\displaystyle\iff\forall x\in dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}).\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(x),(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})(x))\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\tau_{y})

\displaystyle\implies\forall x\in dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}).\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(x),(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})(x))\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(y),\tau_{y})

(by Lemma
31
and
\forall x\in dom(\Gamma^{\prime}).R(x)=R^{\prime}(x)
)

\displaystyle\implies\forall x\in dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}).\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(x),(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})(x))\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(y),\tau)

(by Lemma
32
and the assumption
2
)

\displaystyle\iff\forall x\in dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}).\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(x),(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})(x))

\displaystyle\qquad\land\forall x\in dom(y:\tau^{\prime}_{y},x^{\prime}:\tau^{\prime}_{x}).\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(x),(y\COL\tau^{\prime}_{y},x^{\prime}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{x})(x))

(by the assumption
3
and Lemma
35
)

\displaystyle\iff\forall x\in dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau^{\prime}_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{x}).\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(x),(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau^{\prime}_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{x})(x))

\displaystyle\iff\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau^{\prime}_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{x}))

The proof of
2
.

From Lemma
37
, it is sufficient to show the following:

•

If

1.

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})
and

2.

(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\})\vdash\tau^{\prime}+\tau_{x}\approx\tau_{y}
and

3.

(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\})\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{y}\approx(\tau^{\prime})^{=x^{\prime}}
and

4.

(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,\neq\,0\})\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{y}\approx\tau_{y}
and

5.

x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})\cup dom(R)
and

6.

R(y)=(a,i)\in dom(H)
and

7.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\models r\,>\,\mathbf{0}

then
\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(y))\},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x})
.

Let
\Gamma
be
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}
be
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x}
and
R^{\prime}
be
\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(y))\}
.
Since only the types of
x
and
y
differ between
\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
, we will consider the
\mathbf{SATv}
of
x
and
y
.
By
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,i),\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))
,

\forall j.\llbracket[j/z]r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0\implies(a,i+j)\in dom(H)\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau_{y})
.
Focusing on the case where
j=0
, from the assumption
2

and Lemma
35
, it follows that

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,i)),[0/z]\,\tau^{\prime})\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,i)),\tau_{x})
.
Similarly, focusing on the case where
j\neq 0
,

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{y})
holds.
Furthermore, by Lemma
31
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(y)),\tau_{x})

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(y)),\tau_{x})

(by Lemma
31
)

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(x),\Gamma^{\prime}(x))

(by
\Gamma^{\prime}(x)=\tau_{x}
and
H(R(y))=R^{\prime}(x)
)

About
\mathbf{SATv}
of
y
,

\displaystyle\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}.j=0\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\land j\neq 0\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{y})

\displaystyle\implies\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}.j=0\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\land j\neq 0\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{y})

(by Lemma
31
)

We focus on the case where
j=0
.
If
[0/z]\,\tau^{\prime}
is an array type,
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i)),[0/z]\,\tau^{\prime})\iff\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i)),[0/z]\,((\tau^{\prime})^{=x}))
by definition of
\tau^{=x}
.
If
[0/z]\,\tau^{\prime}
is an integer type,
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i)),[0/z]\,\tau^{\prime})\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i)),[0/z]\,((\tau^{\prime})^{=x}))
because
R^{\prime}(x)=H(R(y))
. In either case,

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i)),[0/z]\,\tau^{\prime})\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i)),[0/z]\,((\tau^{\prime})^{=x}))

(37)

holds. Therefore,

\displaystyle\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}.j=0\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\land j\neq 0\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{y})

\displaystyle\implies\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}.j=0\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,((\tau^{\prime})^{=x}))

\displaystyle\quad\land j\neq 0\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{y})

(by (
37
))

\displaystyle\implies\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}.j=0\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),\tau^{\prime}_{y})

\displaystyle\quad\land j\neq 0\implies\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),[j/z]\,\tau^{\prime}_{y})

(by the assumption
2
and Lemma
33
)

\displaystyle\iff\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}.\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}((a,i+j)),\tau^{\prime}_{y})

Considering the equation above together with
\left\llbracket{r}\right\rrbracket_{R}=\left\llbracket{r}\right\rrbracket_{R^{\prime}}
,
it follows that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(x),\Gamma(x))
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},(a,i),\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))
holds,
if
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(a,i),\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr}))
, that is, If

\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma)
, then

\mathbf{SAT}(H,R^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime})
.

The proof of
3
.

From Lemma
37
, it is sufficient to show the following:

•

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H}\{(a,i)\hookleftarrow\mathit{R}(y)\},\mathit{R},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\left[x\hookleftarrow\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})\right]\left[y\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{y}\right])
holds if

1.

\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma_{1})
and

2.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(x)=\Pi z.(\tau_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})
and

3.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y)=\tau_{y}
and

4.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\approx(\tau^{\prime})^{=y}
and

5.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,\neq\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\approx\tau_{\ast x}
and

6.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\models r\,=\,\mathbf{1}
and

7.

R(x)=(a,i)\in dom(H)

8.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{y}+\tau^{\prime}\approx\tau_{y}

Let

\displaystyle\qquad\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}(pv\boxplus\mathit{R}(z)\boxplus j),[j/w^{\prime}]\,\tau_{\ast x})

\displaystyle\qquad\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y))

\displaystyle\iff\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}\land\llbracket[(j-\mathit{R}(z))/w^{\prime}]{r}_{x}\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}}>0.\implies pv\boxplus j\in dom(H)

\displaystyle\qquad\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}(pv\boxplus j),[(j-z)/w^{\prime}]\,\tau_{\ast x})

\displaystyle\qquad\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y))

\displaystyle\iff\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}\land\left\llbracket{[j/w^{\prime}][(w^{\prime}-R(z))/w^{\prime}]r_{x}}\right\rrbracket_{R}>0.

\displaystyle\qquad\implies pv\boxplus j\in dom(H)\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}(pv\boxplus j),[j/w^{\prime}]\,[(w^{\prime}-z)/w^{\prime}]\,\tau_{\ast x})

\displaystyle\qquad\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y))

\displaystyle\iff\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\Pi w^{\prime}.[(w^{\prime}-z)/w^{\prime}](\tau_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{x}}))\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\Pi w.(\tau_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r}_{y}}))

\displaystyle\iff\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\Pi w^{\prime}.[(w^{\prime}-z)/w^{\prime}](\tau^{\prime}_{\ast x}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{x}}))\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\Pi w.(\tau^{\prime}_{\ast y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5pt{r^{\prime}}_{y}}))

(by the assumption
6
and Lemma
33
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(x),\Gamma^{\prime}(x))\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\Gamma^{\prime}(y))

(by an argument similar to first five equations)

Since
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(x),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(x))\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y))
hold when
\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma_{1})
holds,

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(x),\Gamma^{\prime}(x))\land\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\Gamma^{\prime}(y))
follows.

The proof of
9
.

From Lemma
37
, it is sufficient to show the following:

•

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},x^{\prime}\COL\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})))
holds if

1.

\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma_{1})
and

2.

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},z\COL\mathbf{int}\models\textit{Empty}({\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})})
and

3.

x^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cup dom(R)

This follows immediately from the
Empty
constraint.
∎

Proof of
\cref
lem:preservation.

Proof.

Induction on the derivation of
\left\langle{\mathit{R},\mathit{H},e}\right\rangle\longrightarrow_{D}\left\langle{\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H^{\prime}},e^{\prime}}\right\rangle
.

Case

Rs-Context
:

•

e=E[e_{1}]
,

•

e^{\prime}=E[e_{1}^{\prime}]
,

•

\Theta\mid\Gamma\vdash E[e_{1}]\COL\tau\Rightarrow\Gamma^{\prime}
,

•

\Theta\vdash D
,

•

\mathbf{ConOwn}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
,

•

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
, and

•

\left\langle{R,H,e_{1}}\right\rangle\rightarrow_{D}\left\langle{R^{\prime},H^{\prime},e_{1}^{\prime}}\right\rangle
,

for some
E,e_{1},e_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma
and
\Theta
.
We are to show that there exists
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}
such that

•

\Theta\mid\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\vdash E[e_{1}^{\prime}]\COL\tau\Rightarrow\Gamma^{\prime}
,

•

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H^{\prime},R^{\prime},\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})

•

\mathbf{SAT}(R^{\prime},H^{\prime},E[e_{1}^{\prime}])
.

From
\Theta\mid\Gamma\vdash E[e_{1}]\COL\tau\Rightarrow\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\cref
lem:typing-inversion-context,
there exists
\Gamma_{2},\Gamma_{3},\tau^{\prime}
and fresh
z
such that

•

\Theta\mid\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\COL\tau^{\prime}\Rightarrow\Gamma_{2}
, and

•

\Theta\mid\Gamma_{2},(z\COL\tau^{\prime})\vdash E[z]\COL\tau\Rightarrow\Gamma^{\prime}
.

From
\Theta\mid\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\COL\tau^{\prime}\Rightarrow\Gamma_{2}
, and
\Theta\vdash D
and
\mathbf{ConOwn}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
and
\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
,
we have
\vdash_{D}\left\langle{H,R,e_{1}}\right\rangle\COL\tau^{\prime}\Rightarrow\Gamma_{2}
, and therefore

\vdash_{D}\left\langle{H,R,e_{1}^{\prime}}\right\rangle\COL\tau^{\prime}\Rightarrow\Gamma_{2}
from I.H.
Then, there exists
\Gamma_{3}
such that

•

\Theta\mid\Gamma_{3}\vdash e_{1}^{\prime}\COL\tau^{\prime}\Rightarrow\Gamma_{2}
,

•

\Theta\vdash D
,

•

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma_{3})
,

•

\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma_{3})
.

From
\Theta\mid\Gamma_{2},(z\COL\tau^{\prime})\vdash E[z]\COL\tau\Rightarrow\Gamma^{\prime}
and

\Theta\mid\Gamma_{3}\vdash e_{1}^{\prime}\COL\tau^{\prime}\Rightarrow\Gamma_{2}
and
the freshness of
z
and

\cref
lem:substitution-context-typing, we have

\Theta\mid\Gamma_{3}\vdash E[e_{1}^{\prime}]\COL\tau\Rightarrow\Gamma^{\prime}
.
Set
\Gamma_{3}
to
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}
.

Case

Rs-LetInt
:

•

e=\LET x=n\IN e_{1}
and
e^{\prime}=[x^{\prime}/x]e_{1}
,

•

R^{\prime}=R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto n}\right\}
and
H^{\prime}=H
,

•

\left\langle{R,H,\LET x=n\IN e_{1}}\right\rangle\rightarrow_{D}\left\langle{R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto n}\right\},H,[x^{\prime}/x]e_{1}}\right\rangle
,

•

\Theta\mid\Gamma\vdash\LET x=n\IN e_{1}\COL\tau\Rightarrow\Gamma^{\prime}
,

•

\Theta\vdash D
,

•

\mathbf{ConOwn}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
, and

•

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
,

for some
x,n,e_{1},\Theta,\Gamma
and fresh
x^{\prime}
.
We are to prove that there exists
\Gamma_{p},\tau^{\prime}
such that

•

\Theta\mid\Gamma_{p}\vdash[x^{\prime}/x]e_{1}\COL\tau\Rightarrow(\Gamma^{\prime},x^{\prime}\COL\tau^{\prime})
,

•

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R,\Gamma_{p})
, and

•

\mathbf{SAT}(H,R,\Gamma_{p})
.

From
\cref
lem:inversion-expression-typing and
\Theta\mid\Gamma\vdash\LET x=n\IN e_{1}\COL\tau\Rightarrow\Gamma^{\prime}
,
we have

•

\Gamma\leq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,

•

\Theta\mid\Gamma_{1},x\COL\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\nu=n}\right\}\vdash e_{1}\COL\tau\Rightarrow(\Gamma^{\prime},x\COL\tau^{\prime\prime})

for some
\Gamma_{1},\tau^{\prime\prime}
.
From
\Theta\mid\Gamma_{1},x\COL\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\nu=n}\right\}\vdash e_{1}\COL\tau\Rightarrow(\Gamma^{\prime},x\COL\tau^{\prime\prime})
and
freshness of
x^{\prime}
, we have

\Theta\mid\Gamma_{1},x^{\prime}\COL\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\nu=n}\right\}\vdash[x^{\prime}/x]e_{1}\COL\tau\Rightarrow(\Gamma^{\prime},x^{\prime}\COL\tau^{\prime\prime})
.
Set
\Gamma_{1},x^{\prime}\COL\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\nu=n}\right\}
to
\Gamma_{p}
and
\tau^{\prime\prime}
to
\tau^{\prime}
.

•

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto n}\right\},\Gamma_{p})
follows from
\mathbf{ConOwn}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
and
\cref
item:conown-irrevalent-to-integer-extension of
\cref
lem:conown-irrevalent-to-integer.

•

\mathbf{SAT}(H,R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto n}\right\},\Gamma_{p})
follows from
\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
and
\models[\mathit{R}\{x^{\prime}\mapsto n\}]\,[n/\nu](\nu\,=\,n)
and
\cref
lem:sat-extension. Therefore, we have
\vdash_{D}\left\langle{H^{\prime},R^{\prime},e^{\prime}}\right\rangle\COL\tau\Rightarrow\Gamma^{\prime}
as required.

and freshness of
x^{\prime}
,
we have

\Theta\mid\Gamma_{1},y\COL\tau_{y}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x}^{\prime}\vdash[x^{\prime}/x]e_{1}\COL\tau\Rightarrow(\Gamma^{\prime},x^{\prime}\COL\tau^{\prime\prime\prime})
.

\Gamma_{1},y\COL\tau_{y}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x}^{\prime}
to
\Gamma_{p}
and
\tau^{\prime\prime\prime}
to
\tau^{\prime\prime}
.

•

\mathbf{ConOwn}(H,R\left\{{x^{\prime}\mapsto R(y)}\right\},(\Gamma_{1},y\COL\tau_{y}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2},x^{\prime}\COL\tau_{x}^{\prime}))
follows from

and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\tau_{y}\leq\tau^{\prime}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},y\COL\tau_{y},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\tau^{\prime}\approx\tau^{\prime}_{y}+\tau^{\prime}_{x}
and

\cref
lem:conown-preservation.

we have

follows from

and

\models[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{R}(x)/\nu](\varphi\wedge x\,\leq\,0)
.

D.2.2
Proof of
\cref
lem:progress

Lemma 41
.

\models[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi
holds if
\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
and

\Gamma\vdash\varphi\mbox{ ok}
and

\Gamma\models\varphi
.

Proof.

Induction on the structure of
\Gamma
. If
\Gamma
is empty, then

\displaystyle\Gamma\models\varphi

\displaystyle\iff\models\textit{fml}({\bullet})\implies\varphi

\displaystyle\iff\models\varphi

\displaystyle\iff\models[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi

Therefore,
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi
holds under
\Gamma\models\varphi
.

Next, we show the induction cases.
If
\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},x\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi^{\prime}\}
,
then (1)
\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
is equivalent to

\models[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{R}(x)/\nu]\varphi^{\prime}\wedge\forall z\in dom(\Gamma)\setminus\{x\}.\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(z),\Gamma(z))
; and (2)

\models\textit{fml}({\Gamma})\implies\varphi
is equivalent to

\models\textit{fml}({\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\wedge[x/\nu]\varphi^{\prime}\implies\varphi
.
We need to show
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi
.
From
\forall z\in dom(\Gamma)\setminus\{x\}.\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(z),\Gamma(z))
for any
y\in dom(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y)=\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{y}\}
,
we have
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(y),\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(y))
, which implies
\models[\mathit{R}]\,([\mathit{R}(y)/\nu]\varphi_{y})
.
Since
\textit{fml}({\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
is the conjunction of such formulae, its interpretation under
[R]
must also be true.
That is,
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
holds.
From
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(x),\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi^{\prime}\})
, we directly have
\models[\mathit{R}]\,([\mathit{R}(x)/\nu]\varphi^{\prime})
.

\displaystyle\models\textit{fml}({\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\wedge[x/\nu]\varphi^{\prime}\implies\varphi

\displaystyle\implies\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\wedge[\mathit{R}]\,[x/\nu]\varphi^{\prime}\implies[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi

\displaystyle\implies\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\wedge[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{R}(x)/\nu]\varphi^{\prime}\implies[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi

\displaystyle\implies\models[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi

(by
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
and
\models[\mathit{R}]\,([\mathit{R}(x)/\nu]\varphi^{\prime})
)

If
\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},x\COL\Pi z.(\tau\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})
,
then (1)
\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
is equivalent to
\forall z\in dom(\Gamma)\setminus\{x\}.\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(z),\Gamma(z))

and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(x),\Gamma(x))
; and (2)

\models\textit{fml}({\Gamma})\implies\varphi
is equivalent to

\models\textit{fml}({\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\implies\varphi
.
We need to show
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi
.
From
\forall z\in dom(\Gamma)\setminus\{x\}.\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(z),\Gamma(z))
, we have
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
in the same manner
when
\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},x\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi^{\prime}\}
.

\displaystyle\models\textit{fml}({\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\implies\varphi

\displaystyle\implies\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\implies[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi

\displaystyle\implies\models[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi

(by
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
)

∎

Proof of
\cref
lem:progress.

Proof.

Suppose
e
is not a variable.
From
\vdash_{D}\left\langle{H,R,e}\right\rangle\COL\tau\Rightarrow\Gamma^{\prime}
, we
have

•

\Theta\mid\Gamma\vdash e:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
,

•

\Theta\vdash D
,

•

\mathbf{ConOwn}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
,

•

\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
,

for some
\Theta
and
\Gamma
. The proof goes by induction on the
derivation of

\Theta\mid\Gamma\vdash e:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
. We
conduct a case analysis based on the last rule that derives

\Theta\mid\Gamma\vdash e:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
.
The only non-trivial cases are
T-Assert
,
T-Deref
and
T-Assign
.

Case

T-Assert
:
we have

•

e=\mathbf{assert}(\varphi)\SEQ e_{{\mathrm{0}}}
,

•

\Gamma\models\varphi
,

•

\Theta\mid\Gamma\vdash e_{{\mathrm{0}}}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
,

for some
\varphi
and
e_{0}
.
We are to prove
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi
, which follows from
\cref
lem:sat-soundness.

Case

T-Deref
:
we have

•

e=\LET x=\ast y\IN e_{{\mathrm{0}}}
,

•

\Gamma(y)=\Pi z.(\tau_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})
,

•

\Gamma,z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}+\tau_{x}\approx\tau_{y}
,

•

\Gamma,x\COL\tau_{x},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{y}\approx(\tau^{\prime})^{=x}
,

•

\Gamma,x\COL\tau_{x},z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,\neq\,0\}\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{y}\approx\tau_{y}
,

•

\Gamma,z\COL\{\nu:\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,0\}\models r\,>\,\mathbf{0}
,

•

\Theta\mid\Gamma\left[y\hookleftarrow\Pi z.(\tau^{\prime}_{y}\TREF^{\hskip 0.5ptr})\right],x\COL\tau_{x}\vdash e_{{\mathrm{0}}}:\tau\produces(\Gamma^{\prime},x\COL\tau^{\prime\prime})

for some
x
,
y
,
z
,
\tau^{\prime}
,
\tau^{\prime\prime}
,
\tau_{x}
,
\tau_{y}
,
\tau^{\prime}_{y}
,
r
and
e_{0}
.
We are to prove
\mathit{R}(y)\in dom(H)
.
Because
\Gamma\models[0/z]\,r\,>\,\mathbf{0}
and
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\textit{fml}({\Gamma})
from well-formedness,

\llbracket r\rrbracket_{\mathit{R}\{z\mapsto 0\}}\,>\,0
.
From this and
\mathbf{SAT}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
,
R(y)=(a,k)\in dom(H)
holds for some address
(a,k)
.

Case

T-Assign
:
This is similar to the case of
T-Deref
.

Appendix E
Description of Benchmark Programs

E.1
Benchmark Programs for RQ1

We used the following benchmark programs in the experiments to answer RQ1:

Init-Matrix

is our motivating example in
\cref
fig:motivatingExample, which initializes a matrix with
0
.
It verifies that every element in the initialized matrix is one.

Indexed-Matrix

initializes a matrix with the value
0
where the length of each inner array varies by index.
It verifies that every element in the initialized matrix is one.

Indexed-Value

constructs a
10\times 10
matrix
such that the 0-th row is filled with
10
, the 1st row with
9
, and so on, with the value decreasing as the row index increases.
It verifies that for any arbitrary position
(i,j)
in the generated matrix, the value is always equal to
10-i
.

Sum-Matrix

adds all the elements in a matrix of non-negative integers.
It verifies that the resulting sum is non-negative.

Copy-Matrix

copies the elements of one matrix to another.
It verifies that the copied matrix is identical to the original matrix.

Add-Matrix

takes two integer matrices of the same shape and computes their element-wise sum into a third matrix.
It verifies that each element in the resulting matrix is the sum of the corresponding elements from the two input matrices.

Trace-Matrix

calculates the trace of a matrix of non-negative integers and verifies that the result is non-negative.

Trans-Matrix

computes the transpose of a matrix and verifies that each element is correctly positioned.

Swap

swaps two rows within a matrix and verifies that the values are correctly updated.

Eta-Equ-Sum

computes the sum of all elements in a matrix using two different methods—one that sums elements of each row from left to right before aggregating the row sums, and another that sums elements of each row from right to left before aggregating the row sums—and verifies that both methods yield the same result.

Eta-Equ-Trace

computes the trace of a matrix using two different methods—one that sums elements from the top-left to the bottom-right and another from the bottom-right to the top-left—and verifies that both methods yield the same result.

Lower-Triangle

generates a lower triangular matrix and verifies that all elements above the main diagonal are zero.

Compare-Element

performs an element-wise comparison of two different arrays.

Row-Add

creates an array containing the sum of each row from a matrix.

Boomerang

reads a value from a matrix and immediately writes it back to the same position.

Share-Add-Matrix

generates two pointers to the same matrix and computes their element-wise sum into a third matrix.
It verifies that each element in the resulting matrix is twice the value in the original matrix.

E.2
Benchmark Programs for RQ2

The benchmark programs used in the experiments for RQ2 are as follows:

Init-
n

initializes an array of length
n
with
0
and verifies that the array is successfully initialized.

Sum

computes the sum of an array. It verifies that the sum of a constant array is computed correctly.

Sum-Back

computes the sum of an array starting from the end. It verifies that the sum of a constant array is computed correctly.

Sum-Both

computes the sum of an array by alternately summing elements from the beginning and the end. It verifies that the sum of a constant array is computed correctly.

Sum-Div

computes the sum of an array by splitting the array into two regions,
recursively computing the sum of each region, and adding the obtained results.
It verifies that the sum is computed correctly.

Copy-Array

copies the elements of one array to another. It verifies that the copied array is identical to the original array.

Add-Array

computes the element-wise sum of two arrays of the same size and verifies that the resulting array contains the correct sums.
